## ✅ RECORD 29 — SOTO BETAWI (`indonesia-soto-betawi`) · main · BANKED

**Gates:** `node --check` ✅ · `wowcheck` batch ✅ · `merge` 28 + 1 = 29 ✅ ·
`pricecheck` exact 76 · absent 0 · wrong-product 0 ✅ · `costcheck` **87/87 ✅** ·
batch merged then `rm`'d, syntax re-checked ✅. Ledger: **29 · f2460ef0140665b0**.

**costPP, all three engine-exact:** R64 · R78 · R92.
⚠️ One caught in the gate and worth recording: the budget fork's addStep said *"increase the coconut
milk to 150ml"* while the delta still carried 120ml — prose and data disagreeing, which is exactly the
class of drift `costcheck` exists for. A `swapIng` was added so the ingredient list actually says it,
and the costPP moved R62 → **R64** to match the engine. **The prose was right and the data was the bug.**

**THE LEAD (new):** ⚖️ **TWO FATS, TWO DIFFERENT FAILURE MODES, AND THE ORDER IS THE WHOLE RECIPE.**
Coconut milk and cow's milk in one bowl is what makes soto betawi itself and it is also what makes it
go wrong, because they break under completely different conditions:
- **Coconut milk** is fat suspended by its own proteins — a hard boil *shears* the emulsion apart into
  an oil slick over thin water and it never comes back. So: gentle quiver, 15 minutes, stirred.
- **Cow's milk** is casein micelles, and what stops them repelling one another is **ACID, not heat**.
  So the dairy goes in **off the heat, at the very end**, and — the instruction everyone breaks —
  ⛔ **the lime never enters the pot.** It goes on the rim of the bowl. Tomato goes in raw, in the bowl.
- **Four stages, and each one has a reason:** water simmer 2 hrs (skim hard in the first 15 min — a
  cloudy broth under coconut milk reads as *dirty*, not creamy) → fry the paste in oil 5–6 min, because
  what makes coriander, cumin, clove, cinnamon and nutmeg taste like themselves is **fat-soluble** and
  will never leave the powder in a watery pot → coconut in for the last 15 → dairy off the heat.
- **The potatoes are fried separately and added at the table.** Boiled in the broth they shed starch,
  cloud the liquid and collapse. The default version's trivia carries the reason the bowl is *wide and
  shallow*: in a deep bowl the potato sinks and goes soft, and it is the only crunch in the dish.
⚠️ Not re-led: staggered per-item doneness (Gado-Gado's) and `pecah minyak` (Rendang's) are both
untouched — the frying stage here is argued from **solubility**, not from oil splitting.

**THE MOAT (register: HUMAN POPULATION GENETICS — unspent):** Jakarta's signature soup contains cow's
milk, and it should be the last dish you would expect there. Lactase **persistence** is the oddity, not
the norm — we are all born making lactase and in most of the world production winds down after weaning;
keeping it is a set of recent mutations that spread in populations with long dairying histories. Around
two-thirds of the world's adults have reduced lactase activity, and East and Southeast Asia sit at the
far end, Indonesia among the highest. Two things resolve it, and the second is useful in a kitchen:
**dairy arrived in a TIN** (evaporated and condensed keep for years unrefrigerated, which is why the
region's dairy habit — coffee, tea, desserts, this soup — is tinned rather than fresh, and why a stall
uses evaporated milk), and **lactose intolerance is a matter of DOSE, not a switch** — most malabsorbers
handle ~12g, about a cup of milk, and far more when it arrives with fat and protein. 60ml through a bowl
of rich soup is a rounding error. ✅ Which makes the coconut-only fork **not a substitution** — it is the
older soup, still how much of Indonesia makes it, and arguably the better bowl. The dairy is the newcomer.
🌱 Signature nudge: the raw tomato wedges are the one component a supermarket does badly and a pot on a
sunny stoep does brilliantly.

**Versions:** 💰 *Soto Betawi Santan* (coconut only, cheap cuts, R64, leads and is cheapest — argued as
the version to cook FIRST because with the dairy gone there is one fragile thing in the pot instead of
two and the acid rules relax completely) · 🏆 *Coconut Early, Dairy Last* (default, R78) · 🫀 *Soto
Betawi Jeroan* (R92, +80g beef heart, evaporated milk — heart is not offal in the liver sense, it has no
filtering function, it is simply muscle that worked continuously for the animal's whole life; the dark
colour is myoglobin, and it needs the full 2 hrs or it is rubber. Evaporated milk is *easier*, not
harder: already heat-treated, so its casein is markedly more stable. ⚠️ evaporated ≠ condensed).
Record diet `["omnivore"]` — the §26 union; this dish cannot honestly be forked vegan.
`crossLinks`: Acar Kuning · Bawang Goreng · Sambal Terasi.

## ✅ RECORD 30 — AYAM POP (`indonesia-ayam-pop`) · main · BANKED

**Gates:** `node --check` ✅ · `wowcheck` batch ✅ · `merge` 29 + 1 = 30 ✅ ·
`pricecheck` exact 77 · absent 0 · wrong-product 0 ✅ · `costcheck` **90/90 ✅** ·
`tinza-doctor` RED **10** (floor) · batch merged then `rm`'d, syntax re-checked ✅.
Ledger: **30 · 1d536c480be41955**.

**costPP, all three engine-exact:** R30 · R56 · R61. (v3 authored R60, engine said R61 — corrected to
the engine per §30.1 before merging. costPP is DERIVED.)

**THE LEAD (new, and the inverse of every frying instruction in the lane):**
⚖️ **THE ANTI-BROWNING FRY.** Maillard needs three conditions at once — a **dry** surface, well above
140°C, and time. A piece straight out of a braise has none of them: it is saturated, so every joule the
oil delivers goes into boiling that water off and the surface stays **pinned at ~100°C** until it dries.
That is the loophole the whole dish lives in. In wet, out at **30–45 seconds**, before the surface dries.
No skill — only nerve and a clock.
- ⚠️ **The cue is ACOUSTIC, not visual:** while the bubbling is loud you are safe; the moment it
  *slackens*, the surface water is gone and browning starts within seconds. Watch the clock, not the colour.
- **Skin OFF** — it browns first (fat + collagen, dries ahead of the meat) and braised-then-flash-fried
  skin is the worst of both: too wet to crisp, too cooked to be soft. One of the few chicken dishes
  improved by skinning.
- ⛔ **THE CARTON TRAP:** most SA coconut water has added sugar or juice. Sugar on the surface browns in
  about eight seconds *however wet it is* — it would undo the dish in the last forty seconds of cooking.
  Buy unsweetened, read the panel. The budget fork's 3g sugar limit is written with the same warning.
- **Nothing in the braise colours:** no kecap manis, no soy, no turmeric, no palm sugar, no tamarind —
  pointed out explicitly, because every other Indonesian chicken braise has at least one.
- **Reduce the braise to 60ml.** A third of the flavour is in the liquid most people bin.
- ✅ **Doneness is checked BEFORE the fry, never after** (74°C) — the fry contributes nothing to
  doneness, and judging this dish by eye is the one thing that does not work.

**THE MOAT (register: SENSORY SCIENCE / PSYCHOPHYSICS — unspent, and it explains the dish):** somebody
at your table will quietly wonder whether pale chicken is cooked, and that doubt is a well-studied
feature of how humans taste. Colour arrives before flavour and measurably changes what the mouth reports.
The Bordeaux experiment of 2001: fifty-four oenology students described a white wine, then the *same*
white wine dyed red with an odourless tasteless colourant — and reached overwhelmingly for red-wine
vocabulary on a wine they had described in white-wine terms minutes earlier. Trained tasters, overridden
by a dye. Same effect everywhere it is looked for: a lime drink dyed orange is identified as orange, and
pale food is consistently rated blander than identical browned food. Which makes ayam pop quietly
radical — **brown crust is the most reliable visual promise of savoury flavour a cook can make, and this
dish refuses to make it.** Padang cooking relocates every scrap of colour to the sambal beside it and
lets the pale bird be the resting place between them. The intensity is not missing; it has been moved.
🌱 Nudge: the chilli in the sambal is a perennial in a Pretoria garden and crops for years off one pot.

**Versions:** 💰 *Ayam Pop Air Biasa* (plain water, R30, leads and is cheapest — honest that coconut
water is nearly half the cost, and honest that it is **not** what makes the chicken pale; that is
entirely the wet-surface trick. With no sugar in the pot at all this fork is the *more forgiving* fry) ·
🏆 *Coconut Water Braise, Forty-Second Fry* (default, R56 — its trivia: coconut water is sterile inside
an unopened nut and close enough to blood plasma in mineral balance to have been used as an emergency IV
fluid in isolated documented cases; stated as a coincidence of chemistry, **not** a health claim and not
a thing to attempt) · 🌶️ *with Sambal Lado Mudo* (R61 — the green sambal is **cooked BEFORE it is
pounded**, the opposite order to Sambal Terasi, which softens the raw grassy edge and keeps it bright
green; the card defers the mortar laws to Sambal Terasi by name rather than re-teaching them).
Record diet `["omnivore"]`. `crossLinks`: Rendang · Sambal Terasi · Acar Kuning — Rendang and Ayam Pop
are both Minangkabau, so that link is a real one rather than a filler.

## ✅ RECORD 31 — TEMPE MENDOAN (`indonesia-tempe-mendoan`) · starter · BANKED

**Gates:** `node --check` ✅ · `wowcheck` batch ✅ · `merge` 30 + 1 = 31 ✅ ·
`pricecheck` exact 78 · absent 0 · wrong-product 0 ✅ · `costcheck` **93/93 ✅** ·
batch merged then `rm`'d, syntax re-checked ✅. Ledger: **31 · aa984fda62ddbc3c**.

**costPP, all three engine-exact:** R51 · R77 · R79.

**⚠️ THE ANGLE-REUSE RISK, NAMED AND HANDLED IN THE CARD ITSELF.** Ayam Pop (record 30) also finishes
pale, so this card opens by drawing the distinction out loud rather than hoping nobody notices: **there,
the pale finish is a WET SURFACE holding the temperature down; here the oil is very hot and the surface
is not especially wet — what stops it crisping is the FLOUR.** Two pale results, two different mechanisms,
and the reader is told so in the second paragraph.

**THE LEAD (new):** ⚖️ **RICE FLOUR IS WHAT MAKES UNDER-FRYING LEGAL.** Rice flour and cornflour carry
no gluten. A wheat batter builds a gluten network the moment it is mixed and needs *complete* cooking to
stop being dough — pulled early it is raw batter, pasty and adhesive. Rice starch gelatinises and sets
into a tender cohesive film very early and simply **stays** that way, with nothing raw about it, long
before it dries and turns brittle. **So "soft" is a real destination in a rice-flour batter and does not
exist at all in a wheat one.** If you only have plain flour, make the crisp fork instead — the card says
so rather than letting someone make it badly.
- **The slice thickness is calculated BACKWARDS from the batter's clock.** The batter survives ~90
  seconds before it crisps, so the tempeh must be 4–5mm and wide enough to be fully hot in that same 90
  seconds. A 15mm slab gives a cold raw centre in a cooked coat. Geometry, not preference.
- ⛔ **THERE IS A RIGHT AND A WRONG KIND OF SOFT, AND THEY LOOK IDENTICAL GOING IN.** Pale-and-soft from
  a short fry at 180°C is tender and dry inside. Pale-and-soft from a long fry in lukewarm oil is a
  grease sponge — oil enters food only when the outward rush of steam is too weak to keep it out, and
  weak steam is exactly what cool oil produces. **Hot and quick, or you get the failure this dish is
  usually accused of.** This is why the oil volume is 400ml, matching the tahu-gejrot / kalasan deep-fry
  precedent rather than the 15–40ml shallow-fry lines.
- **Batter thin enough to see the spoon through**, and re-whisked before every batch — rice flour settles
  to a hard sludge within ten minutes, so a standing batter is water on top and paste underneath.

**THE MOAT (register: MANUSCRIPT HISTORY / an argument from silence — unspent):** tempeh is a staple with
**no known inventor and almost no history.** The earliest secure written mention is in the *Serat
Centhini*, an early-nineteenth-century Javanese manuscript, and even there it appears casually as an
ordinary thing needing no explanation. Before that, essentially nothing — and Java is not short of
records; it has centuries of temple inscriptions and trade lists itemising market goods and tribute in
fussy detail. A food that important leaving no trace is a **loud absence.** The likeliest reason is that
it simply is not very old: tempeh needs soybeans in quantity, soy is not native to Java, and large-scale
cultivation there is bound up with the growth of Chinese communities and the soy trade. Which leaves the
least dramatic origin as the most probable — somebody left boiled beans wrapped in a warm corner, came
back to a firm white cake, and was curious enough to eat it. Second beat, and you can taste this one:
**mendoan is named after being undercooked** — Banyumasan *mendo* = half-done. Almost every food name
advertises its ingredient, its home or its quality; this one advertises its own undercookedness, which
tells you the pale floppy result was the point from the beginning.
⚠️ Deliberately untouched: fermentation-as-nutrition and the B12 half-myth are **Tempe Goreng's** and are
not referenced at all. This card's moat is documentary, not microbiological.
🌱 Nudge, SA-specific and useful: coriander bolts the moment a Pretoria summer gets hot — sow it autumn
and winter, keep it in afternoon shade, and cut the whole young plant at the base rather than picking
leaves. Treat it as a fast crop, not a permanent plant.

**Versions:** 💰 *Mendoan Rumahan* (shallow pan, chilli only, R51, leads and is cheapest — and honest that
frying oil at full depth is close to a quarter of the plate cost, which is why household versions of
street food are shallow everywhere in the world: a vendor's oil does hundreds of jobs, yours does one.
The trade-off is stated as **money swapped for attention** — one fritter at a time, wait between batches,
turn twice) · 🏆 *Rice Flour, Ninety Seconds, Pale* (default, R77 — trivia: Banyumas ferments tempeh
specially in thin wide sheets for this dish, which is why mendoan made elsewhere drifts thicker and gets
fried longer, and **if a recipe says fry until golden it has already travelled too far**) · 🟡 *Tempe
Kemul* (R79, the Wonosobo cousin — thicker, turmeric-yellow, fried genuinely crisp at a LOWER 165–170°C
so the thickness sets before the outside browns. Two towns, one tempeh, one flour, two opposed doctrines
about when to stop — and it is also the fork to make if you only have wheat flour, since a fully fried
batter has no raw-dough problem).
Record diet `["vegan"]` — all three forks vegan, the §26 union.
`crossLinks`: Tempe Goreng · Sambal Terasi · Sayur Asem (record 26 now earning inbound links).

**⛔ STORAGE IS A REFUSAL, DELIBERATELY.** The card says plainly that mendoan does not keep, rather than
giving a reheating plan that produces a disappointing snack — reheated, it crisps, which is the one thing
it was made not to do. What *does* keep is everything before the oil: sliced tempeh 3 days, the dry flour
and spice mix a week in a jar, greens added only at frying time.

## ✅ RECORD 32 — SAMBAL MATAH (`indonesia-sambal-matah`) · staple · BANKED

**Gates:** `node --check` ✅ · `wowcheck` batch ✅ · `merge` 31 + 1 = 32 ✅ ·
`pricecheck` exact 80 · absent 0 · wrong-product 0 ✅ · `costcheck` **96/96 ✅** ·
`tinza-doctor` RED **10** (floor) and **check 13 green** — 1153 records measured through the real
`wkEffectiveMult`, nothing inflated past 3×. ⚠️ That check matters for this record specifically: it
declares `type: [... "sambal", "condiment" ...]`, which is what stops the portion brain rescaling a
condiment the way it once rendered Sambal Terasi as *160g of shrimp paste at R529*.
Batch merged then `rm`'d, syntax re-checked ✅. Ledger: **32 · a059187851b6a31f**.

**costPP, all three engine-exact:** R13 · R16 · R24.

**⚠️ THE HARD PART OF THIS CARD WAS FINDING A LEAD NOBODY OWNED**, because matah sits surrounded:
mortar-vs-blender and terasi toasting are **Sambal Terasi's**, cook-then-pound is **Ayam Pop's lado
mudo**, allium cutting chemistry is **Acar Kuning's**, salt-draws-its-own-dressing is **Rujak's**, and
draw-and-discard is **Acar's** again. Four of those are referenced by name and deferred to rather than
re-taught. The card cites Acar and Sambal Terasi explicitly in-method.

**THE LEAD (new, and specific to this dish alone):** ⚖️ **EVERY AROMATIC IN THIS BOWL IS SOMETHING YOU
WOULD NORMALLY FISH OUT AND THROW AWAY — AND HERE YOU EAT ALL OF IT.** Lemongrass and lime leaf are
infusion aromatics everywhere else in the file: bruised, simmered, removed. In matah they are chewed. A
thing you chew has to be cut so it is chewable, and **each one needs a different cut**:
- **Lemongrass** — bottom third only, peeled until a test slice **BENDS instead of SNAPPING** (that is
  the go/no-go test), then sliced **ACROSS the grain at under 1mm**. The fibre runs the length of the
  stalk in long tough bundles, so a 2mm slice is splinters and a 0.5mm slice is soft. This is the single
  thing that ruins a home-made matah, and at double quantity (v3) the peeling standard doubles with it.
- **Lime leaf / lime zest** — central rib out, rolled, shredded hair-fine.
- **Shallot** — 1mm rings, and a **sharp** knife, because a blunt blade crushes rather than cuts and
  gives a wetter, harsher sambal from the identical bulb.
- **The chillies are sliced, not crushed**, so the heat sits in discrete rings and arrives as bright
  flashes against sweet shallot rather than as a uniform burn — which is why matah eats hotter than its
  chilli weight suggests.
- **Tossed with fingertips, never a spoon.** Distributing, not combining.
- **The hot oil is the only heat and it is not there to cook** — it takes the raw squeak off the shallot
  and carries flavour across the bowl. Coconut oil is worth it because it firms as it stands, which is
  where matah's slightly clinging texture comes from rather than a puddle at the bottom.

**⚠️ THE HONEST SA CALL, made in-method rather than hidden:** kaffir lime leaves are a PRICE LANDMINE
(`lime leaves` → `lime` R140, wrong) and are genuinely hard to find here, so they are **in-method only**
per the standing rule. The card does not simply drop the citrus — it gives **pared lime zest, shredded
hair-fine**, as the honest route, says plainly that it is not identical (lime leaf has a floral note zest
cannot reach), and ⛔ explicitly forbids substituting an ordinary bay leaf, which is a different plant.
The substitute is eaten and cut the same way, so the lead survives intact.

**THE MOAT (register: GEOLOGY / soil science — unspent):** the shallots that are most of this bowl grow
in some of the most fertile soil on earth, and the fertility arrives **on a delay**. Indonesia has around
130 active volcanoes, more than any country; Java supports ~150 million people on an island the size of
England largely because of what its volcanoes did to its dirt. But the mechanism is not the obvious one:
**fresh ash is not fertile.** It is essentially powdered glass — sterile, sharp, chemically locked up and
catastrophic for anything under it. Only weathering, over decades and centuries, breaks that glass into
clay minerals with enormous surface area that hold water and nutrients — the soil type called **andosol**,
which is exactly right for the shallow-rooted hungry crops Indonesian agriculture runs on, shallots and
chillies among them. So a farmer planting shallots on a volcano's flank is farming **a disaster that
happened to somebody's great-grandparents**, and the eruption that ruins this season makes the soil
extraordinary for a generation nobody in the field will meet. That trade is why a village stays put
somewhere everyone outside it considers obviously mad.
🌱 Signature nudge, and a strong one for this household: **a shallot multiplies.** Push one healthy bulb
in during autumn, pointy end just proud, and by early summer it has divided into six or eight — lift the
clump, keep the best few, replant. Sun, a little compost, not much water, stores for months in a paper
bag. After the first once-off bulb you never buy them again. Same arithmetic as the heirloom garlic.

**Versions:** 💰 *Sambal Matah Polos* (no terasi, sunflower oil, R13, leads and is cheapest — and stated
**not** to be a compromise: a great many Balinese matah carry no terasi at all, particularly the one
served with grilled fish, on the grounds that the fish already brings the savoury depth) · 🏆 *Sliced
Raw, Hot Oil Over* (default, R16 — trivia: matah is much younger as a famous dish than it looks, carried
out of Bali by the tourism-era restaurant kitchens looking for what was distinctly Balinese rather than
generically Indonesian, and **it barely changed on the way, because its identity is a set of things it
refuses to do and there is no simpler version to drift toward**) · 🍃 *Sambal Matah Lengkap* (R24 —
about a quarter of the bowl by weight is lemongrass and shredded citrus, an amount absurd in any other
dish, and it works because citral is intensely **aromatic but genuinely mild on the palate**; the gap
between how much you can smell and how much you can comfortably eat is the space this version lives in).
Record diet `["omnivore","vegan"]` — the §26 union.
`crossLinks`: Sate Lilit · Ayam Betutu · Sambal Terasi — the first two Balinese, so real links not filler.

**⚠️ STORAGE IS A FADE, NOT A SPOIL, and the card says which:** salt keeps drawing water so the rings go
limp, and the lemongrass gives its volatiles up to the oil, so by day two it is a mild oily shallot relish
that is perfectly pleasant and is not matah. ✅ The fix offered is to **prep ahead, not make ahead** —
slice everything into separate tubs a day early and toss with salt, lime and hot oil in the two minutes
before serving.

## ✅ RECORD 33 — ES TELER (`indonesia-es-teler`) · dessert · BANKED

**Gates:** `node --check` ✅ · `wowcheck` batch ✅ · `merge` 32 + 1 = 33 ✅ ·
`pricecheck` exact 83 · absent 0 · **wrong-product 0** ✅ · `costcheck` **99/99 ✅** ·
batch merged then `rm`'d, syntax re-checked ✅. Ledger: **33 · 1fc6748d75a22cab**.

**costPP, all three engine-exact:** R19 · R27 · R30.

**✅ THE AVOCADO LANDMINE IS CLOSED — MEASURED, NOT ASSUMED.** Memory carried `bare avocado resolves
PER COUNT` and a live `30g avocado` bug in `boerekos-gemsbok-stuffed-fillet`. Both `avocado` R65/kg and
`avocado_each` R13 are live keys, so `100g avocado` was written with care — and pricecheck now reports it
clean, explicitly: *"(\"30g avocado\") is bridged by AVG_WEIGHT_G in core.js and is not a fault."*
**The mirror bridge fixed it.** The carried note about that gemsbok line should be struck from the open
list; it is no longer a bug.

**THE LEAD (new):** ⚖️ **ICE IS AN INGREDIENT, AND EVERY GRAM THAT MELTS IS WATER YOU DID NOT CHOOSE TO
ADD.** There is no cooking in this dish, so everything is decided in the sixty seconds of assembly:
- **Chill every component before anything meets the ice.** Pour room-temperature liquid over ice and the
  ice gets a second job it should never have — instead of *holding* the cold it has to *create* it, and
  the only way ice can do that is by melting. Cold-into-cold and the ice has nothing to do but sit there.
- **Mix the whole liquid in a JUG and taste it there**, because once the glass is built you cannot stir
  it — a spoon turns a ripe avocado into green streaks in three strokes. Same reasoning as salting a soup
  rather than salting the bowls.
- **Make the liquid taste slightly TOO sweet and TOO strong**, because the ice will dilute it by design.
- **Dissolve the palm sugar in hot water and cool it** — grated jaggery will not dissolve cold, ever.
- **Spoon the avocado ragged, never cube it.** It is a TEXTURE, not a fruit: ragged surfaces blur into the
  coconut liquid, cubes stay separate and the glass reads as a fruit salad instead of one thing. And it
  must be properly ripe — nothing else in the glass is strong enough to hide a grassy avocado.
- **Crush the ice at the last second**, with the cost stated: crushing multiplies surface area, melting
  happens only at the surface.
⚠️ **The shaving lead is NOT used** — same block as Japan's Kakigori, no honest home route in SA. The card
says so plainly and sends you to crushed, with the trade explained rather than hidden.
⚖️ **Explicit deference to Es Cendol in the second paragraph:** Cendol is built on keeping syrup and
salted coconut *separate* so every spoonful differs; es teler combines everything so every mouthful is
the same. Two Indonesian iced desserts, two opposed intentions, stated side by side.

**⚠️ TWO NOT-IN-SA CALLS, AND THEY GET DIFFERENT ANSWERS ON PURPOSE:**
- **Young coconut flesh** — NO stand-in, leave it out (the melinjo precedent). Mature coconut is crunchy,
  desiccated is dry fibre, neither is close. ✅ But you *can* buy the water from exactly that nut, so
  `coconut water` does double duty and carries the flavour even though the texture is gone.
- **Ripe jackfruit** — `jackfruit` is ABSENT; `young jackfruit` R108 IS a live key and is a **trap**, so
  the card forbids it by name: that is green unripe jackfruit sold as a meat substitute, it is savoury,
  and it would be genuinely unpleasant in a dessert. `pineapple` stands in for the JOB (sweet, acidic,
  fibrous, year-round) and is stated to be **no substitute at all for the aroma**, which nothing replicates.
- `ice` and `pandan` are both ABSENT keys. Ice is written honestly as **`250ml water`** frozen overnight —
  which prices correctly at R0 and is what ice actually is. Pandan is in-method only, with the grow nudge.

**THE MOAT (register: COMMERCIAL / INDUSTRIAL HISTORY + geometry — unspent):** for most of history ice in
the tropics was not difficult, it was impossible — and then for about eighty years it arrived by sailing
ship from Massachusetts. Frederic Tudor, 1806, loaded a brig with New England pond ice on the reasoning
that it was worthless where it was and priceless where it was not; almost everyone told him it would be
seawater on arrival. By the 1830s the trade reached India, and mid-century ice cut from ponds outside
Boston sold in ports across South and Southeast Asia, packed in **sawdust that the timber mills produced
by the mountain and threw away**. Purpose-built ice houses went up to hold it. ⚖️ **And the whole business
rested on the same geometric fact this recipe rests on:** melting happens only at the SURFACE, so a large
block is insulated by its own bulk — halve every piece and you double the surface area for the same
weight. Tudor was exploiting scale, not insulation. Mechanical refrigeration killed the trade entirely
around 1900 and it is now almost forgotten. *"The next time you decide between cubes and crushed, you are
making the same calculation Tudor made, on a scale of about one glass."*
🌱 Nudge: **pandan** — nearly impossible to buy fresh here, genuinely easy to grow, happy in a large pot
in warm shade, multiplies by offsets, wants cover from a hard Highveld frost. One plant supplies a
household indefinitely and no dried version comes close.

**Versions:** 💰 *Es Teler Santan* (coconut and palm sugar only, **VEGAN**, R19, leads and is cheapest —
and it names the three separate jobs condensed milk was doing, sweetness / thickness / dairy fat, and
replaces each; body is the one that cannot be replaced, so the honest move is **less ice and a smaller
glass** rather than pretending) · 🏆 *Chilled Cold, Assembled at the Last Second* (default, R27,
vegetarian — trivia: avocado in a sweet drink is only surprising depending on where you grew up. It is
botanically a berry and turns up sweet across Brazil, Vietnam, the Philippines and Indonesia; the
**savoury-only convention is the historical outlier**. It works because avocado has almost no sugar, almost
no acid and ~15% fat — nearly the composition of cream, which is exactly the job it is given) ·
🥭 *Es Teler Mangga* (R30 — mango and jackfruit share an ester-driven aroma family with a note near
banana, so mango smells much closer to the real thing than pineapple does; but mango is soft where
jackfruit is fibrous, which is why the pineapple **stays in** — one component with resistance or the whole
glass becomes one uninterrupted soft mouthful. Jaggery drops 20g → 12g because this fork tips into
cloying more easily than any other on the card).
Record diet `["vegan","vegetarian"]` — the §26 union.
`crossLinks`: Es Cendol · Klepon · Rujak.

**⛔ STORAGE IS A REFUSAL AGAIN, and correctly:** assembled, it keeps about four minutes. What keeps is
every component separately — which is how a stall runs it, and it turns a made-ahead dessert into a
two-minute job at the moment somebody wants one. Avocado is the only thing that cannot be prepped.

## ✅ RECORD 34 — AYAM TALIWANG (`indonesia-ayam-taliwang`) · main · BANKED

**Gates:** `node --check` ✅ · `wowcheck` batch ✅ · `merge` 33 + 1 = 34 ✅ ·
`pricecheck` exact 84 · absent 0 · wrong-product 0 ✅ · `costcheck` **102/102 ✅** ·
`tinza-doctor` RED **10** (floor) · batch merged then `rm`'d, syntax re-checked ✅.
Ledger: **34 · f7a91f838f63f11c**.

**costPP, all three engine-exact:** R32 · R36 · R43.

**THE LEAD (new — the cold start said this record needed the PRESS as its lead, and it does):**
⚖️ **A CHICKEN IS THE WRONG SHAPE FOR A FIRE.** The breast is thin, lean and finished at ~65°C; the
thigh is thick worked muscle that only becomes good past 75°C. One fire, two targets, ten degrees and
several centimetres apart — something is always wrong. Butterflying stops treating it as a bird:
- Backbone **out with scissors** (keep it for stock), then **crack the keel bone** — that is what holds
  the A-shape — and a **slash to the bone on the inside of each thigh**, which opens the slowest part.
- Result is **roughly one thickness**, 2.5–3cm, so breast and thigh finish minutes apart instead of
  fifteen, and the whole bird cooks in about half the time because heat travels half as far.
- ⚠️ **Which makes the SIZE of the bird part of the recipe.** Flatten a big broiler and the thigh is
  still 4cm — no amount of butterflying rescues that over direct coals. Smallest bird available, or
  joint it and grill pieces. *"A jointed bird is an honest substitute and a blackened one is not."*
- ✅ **THE WEIGHT IS ABOUT CONTACT, NOT COMPRESSION.** Unweighted, a flat bird touches the grate at a
  few high spots and arches away everywhere else — dark stripes and pale valleys. Pressed, the skin is
  in continuous contact, and contact conducts far faster and more evenly than radiant heat across a gap.
  Same reason a sandwich press browns evenly and a pan does not. It also renders the fat instead of
  steaming it.
- ⚖️ **THE GLAZE IS ARITHMETIC.** The paste is roughly a third sugar and shallot; sugars caramelise then
  burn at ~160–170°C; a grate over hot coals is well past 300°C. So a sugared surface has **seconds** of
  margin while the thigh needs twenty-five minutes. Paste goes on only in the last six, in **two thin
  coats plus a setting coat**, never one thick one (a thick layer stays wet underneath and slides off).
  *"When it smells sharp instead of toasty you have about ten seconds."*
⚖️ **Explicit deference, in-method:** this is stated as the OPPOSITE of Ayam Betutu — a huge volume of
paste before a long cook works there precisely because the heat is low and indirect and never reaches
burning temperature. Sate Ayam keeps fire-building; this card does not re-teach two-zone fires.

**⚠️ NOT-IN-SA CALL:** `kencur` → `galangal` 1:1, applied and credited in-method as Tina's standing ruling
rather than silently substituted.

**THE MOAT (register: MARINE BIOLOGY / reproductive ecology — unspent):** Lombok has the strangest
harvest in Indonesia. One morning in February or March, thousands wade out before dawn to catch **nyale**
— sea worms that surface in enormous numbers for a few hours and then are gone for a year. They are eaten:
fried, grilled in banana leaf, or with chilli and coconut. **The biology is stranger than the festival:
what surfaces is not the animal.** Palolo worms live in reef crevices and grow a specialised rear section
packed with eggs or sperm, and that section **DETACHES and swims to the surface on its own** while the
front half stays safely in the reef and grows a new one for next year. The sea fills with millions of
independently swimming reproductive halves. And the timing is locked to the lunar cycle precisely enough
to be predicted a year ahead to within a day or two — **the Sasak lunar calendar has been calling the
date for generations**, and the same phenomenon is a fixed festival in Samoa and Fiji on a different
month, every population reading its own moon. The Sasak explanation is the Princess Mandalika legend:
she threw herself into the sea rather than choose between her suitors and returned as the worms so
everyone could have her — *"which is, if you think about it, not a bad description of what is actually
happening out there."*
🌱 Nudge: garlic is planted from a clove, not a seed — autumn, pointy end up, 5cm down, and each clove
becomes a bulb. Keep the best back each season and plant your own line indefinitely. One of the few crops
where a single purchase genuinely is the last one.

**Versions:** 💰 *Ayam Taliwang Wajan* (heavy pan, no fire, R32, leads and is cheapest — argued as **more
controllable, not lesser**: a fire has one temperature and you cannot turn it down, and a dial lets you
drop the heat at exactly the moment the dish is most likely to be lost. Honest about what is given up —
**smoke is a real ingredient, not a romantic detail**, and smoked paprika gets a suggestion of it and no
more) · 🏆 *Butterflied, Weighted, Glazed Twice* (default, R36 — trivia: named after **Karang Taliwang, a
neighbourhood in Mataram**, not a region or a people, which is unusual in this file and points at how
local Indonesian food is before it is national; and it marks a boundary — Lombok is noticeably fiercer
than Bali, 35km away and visible from it, with a genuinely different threshold for hot) · 🔥 *Pelalah*
(R43 — double chilli **changes the cooking, not just the eating**: more solids means less water, and
water is what protects a glaze, since evaporating moisture holds the sugars near 100°C. A drier paste
loses that shield sooner, so the window drops from ~4 minutes to ~2. General lesson hiding in a specific
dish: **reducing a glaze to make it cling is also reducing the time you have to work with it**).
Record diet `["omnivore"]`. `crossLinks`: **Plecing Kangkung** (the canonical Lombok partner — a real
pairing, not filler) · Sambal Matah · Ayam Betutu.

## ✅ RECORD 35 — SAYUR LODEH (`indonesia-sayur-lodeh`) · side · BANKED

**Gates:** `node --check` ✅ · `wowcheck` batch ✅ · `merge` 34 + 1 = 35 ✅ ·
`pricecheck` exact 85 · absent 0 · wrong-product 0 ✅ · `costcheck` **105/105 ✅** ·
`tinza-doctor` RED **10** (floor), check 13 green · batch merged then `rm`'d, syntax re-checked ✅.
Ledger: **35 · 2bf0eab60f9d6d0b**.

**costPP, all three engine-exact:** R40 · R48 · R74.

**THE LEAD (new — and the cold start's own warning was that coconut emulsion is Soto Betawi's, so this
card had to find a different argument):** ⚖️ **IN A COCONUT POT THE RUNNING ORDER IS SET BY WHAT EACH
VEGETABLE DOES TO THE COCONUT, NOT BY HOW HARD IT IS.** Hardness-staggering is deferred to Gado-Gado in
one line and not re-taught; the second running order sitting on top of it is the one nobody writes down.
- **What breaks coconut milk is SALT AND ACID, not heat alone.** The proteins holding the fat droplets
  apart work by electrical charge. **Acid** neutralises it; **salt** *screens* it — dissolved ions crowd
  the proteins and mask the charge so the droplets stop repelling. Either one plus heat and it goes.
  ✅ So the two most dangerous things in the pot are the most innocent-looking: **salt goes in at the
  END, anything sour goes in last or not at all.**
- ⚖️ **USE THE COCONUT TWICE, AT TWO STRENGTHS — dilution as protection.** 100ml diluted into the water
  is *santan encer*, the long-simmering cooking liquid, and it survives because there is barely any
  emulsion in it to shear. The 50ml held back undiluted is *santan kental*, stirred in off the heat at
  the end. Two strengths, two jobs, and it is why a proper lodeh tastes of far more coconut than it
  contains. **This is a different mechanism from Soto Betawi's** — that card is about two fats with two
  failure modes; this one is about diluting a single fat to make it survivable.
- ⛔ **LID OFF THE WHOLE TIME.** A lid drips condensate back and, worse, traps heat so the liquid boils
  harder than it looks — and localised boiling against the base is exactly where an emulsion shears. The
  stirring is for even heat, not for sticking.
- ⚠️ **The aubergine is the sneaky one** — it drinks liquid rather than releasing it, so a pot that
  looked right tightens ten minutes later, and a thicker pot heats less evenly and breaks more easily.
  Top up with HOT water, never cold, never more coconut.
- ✅ **The card is honest that a split cannot be fully fixed.** Off the heat, cool 5 minutes, whisk in a
  tablespoon of fresh thick coconut off the heat and the new intact emulsion carries some of the loose
  oil back. *"It will not be as good as one that never split. Serve it and say nothing."*

**⚠️ THE `young jackfruit` KEY IS USED HERE CORRECTLY — and this is the card that redeems it.** Es Teler
(record 33) had to forbid `young jackfruit` R108 by name because that record needed RIPE jackfruit and the
tinned green product would have been a savoury disaster in a dessert. Lodeh is the dish that actually
wants it, so the same key that was a trap two records ago is the right product here — with an in-method
warning that the tins look alike and the mistake is unrecoverable.

**THE MOAT (register: ARCHAEOBOTANY / genetics-as-shipping-record — unspent):** the coconut is one of very
few crops whose DNA reads as a shipping record, and it says the whole thing happened **twice**. Genotyping
well over a thousand palms found two clearly distinct populations — one Pacific, one Indian Ocean — meaning
coconuts were domesticated **independently in two places**, by two groups with no knowledge of each other,
and the difference is still legible in a nut you can buy today. The Pacific type travels with the
Austronesian expansion (a coconut is food, drink, rope, roofing and a container at once — close to a
survival kit for an ocean crossing); the Indian Ocean type moves west along Arab and Indian routes to East
Africa. And the anomaly: **Caribbean-side coconuts carry Pacific ancestry**, fitting Spanish ships bringing
them across from the Philippines on the Manila route. *"A tree's genome recording a specific colonial
shipping lane is not a thing you expect from a soup ingredient."* ⚖️ Best beat last — **coconuts FLOAT**,
and a nut can survive months at sea and germinate on a beach unaided, so the plant's own dispersal was
hiding the human story underneath it. Only genetics could separate the two, and almost everywhere the
coconut matters most, it arrived in a boat with somebody.
🌱 Nudge: baby marrow is the easiest thing to grow in a Pretoria garden and the one most likely to defeat
you by succeeding. *"Plant one. Not three."*

**Versions:** 💰 *Sayur Lodeh Polos* (garden vegetables only, **VEGAN**, R40, leads and is cheapest — and
argued as what lodeh actually IS on most days: **a technique with a shopping list attached loosely**,
which is why no two versions agree. Generous substitution rules, one hard constraint — nothing sour. The
lost terasi is replaced with mushrooms for their free glutamates, and ⛔ explicitly **not** with more
salt, since salt is the thing the whole recipe spends forty minutes protecting the coconut from) ·
🏆 *Thin Coconut First, Thick Coconut Last* (default, R48 — trivia: lodeh's second life in Yogyakarta as
something closer to a civic instruction than a recipe, the palace calling for households across the city
to cook one specified pot simultaneously at times of pestilence. The striking part is the **choice of
dish**: not a ceremony or anything rare, but a cheap everyday pot nearly every household already knew how
to make. An instruction everyone can actually follow, on the same evening, from what is already in the
kitchen) · 🟫 *Sayur Lodeh Komplit* (R74, tempeh and tofu — and they behave **oppositely**: tofu is a
near-flavourless sponge that ABSORBS, so it goes in for the last 2 minutes only and is pressed first to
make room; tempeh is dense and strongly savoury and CONTRIBUTES, so it is fried for colour and added early
to leach that flavour out. Two textures from what looks like one category).
Record diet `["omnivore","vegan"]` — the §26 union.
`crossLinks`: Tempe Goreng · Nasi Uduk · Sayur Asem.

### ⛔ SPENT-LIST ADDITIONS FROM RECORD 31
**Lead:** rice flour has no gluten, so the SOFT stage is a real destination that does not exist in a
wheat batter — under-frying is legal because of the flour · slice thickness derived backwards from the
batter's 90-second clock · the right and wrong kind of soft (hot-and-quick is tender, cool-and-slow is a
grease sponge, because oil enters only when the outward steam is too weak to keep it out) · a rice-flour
batter settles to sludge in ten minutes (Tempe Mendoan). Also spent: *thicker batter needs a LOWER oil
temperature or the outside browns before the inside sets* (Tempe Kemul).
**Moat:** tempeh has no recorded history before ~1814 and the absence is the story — an argument from
silence in a record-heavy island, plus soy's late arrival, which makes an accidental household origin the
most probable one; and mendoan is named after its own undercookedness (register MANUSCRIPT HISTORY).
**Ownership added:** batter chemistry and gluten-vs-rice-starch = Tempe Mendoan · frying-oil economics =
Mendoan Rumahan · the crisp-vs-soft doctrine pair = Tempe Kemul.

### ⛔ SPENT-LIST ADDITIONS FROM RECORD 32
**Lead:** you EAT the infusion aromatics, so each gets its own cut — lemongrass bottom-third, peel until
it bends not snaps, sliced across the grain under 1mm · lime leaf ribbed, rolled, hair-fine · shallot in
1mm rings with a SHARP knife because a blunt one crushes · sliced chillies give discrete flashes of heat
rather than a uniform burn · fingertips not a spoon · hot oil as carrier, not cooking (Sambal Matah).
**Moat:** volcanic fertility arrives on a delay — fresh ash is sterile powdered glass and only weathering
makes andosol, so a farmer is working a disaster from three generations back (register GEOLOGY / soil).
Second beat: citral is hugely aromatic but mild on the palate, which is why a quarter-lemongrass bowl is
edible (Sambal Matah Lengkap).
**Ownership added:** the knife-not-the-mortar sambal = Sambal Matah · lemongrass as an EATEN ingredient =
Sambal Matah · shallot cultivation and multiplication = Sambal Matah · volcanic soil = Sambal Matah.

Seven records in, the binding constraint is no longer finding good Indonesian dishes, it is finding leads
and moat registers nobody owns. Records 30–32 each needed an explicit deference line in-method (Ayam Pop
→ nothing; Mendoan → Ayam Pop's pale-but-different; Matah → Sambal Terasi, Acar, Rujak, lado mudo). That
is working and it reads well — it makes the file feel like one book rather than 32 leaflets — but it is
worth naming: **the deference lines are now a feature of the house voice and should be kept deliberately,
not treated as an apology.**
Registers still unspent: archaeology · music · mathematics/calendrics · cartography · sport ·
architecture · maritime navigation · art history · entomology · textiles · hydrology.

### ⛔ SPENT-LIST ADDITIONS FROM RECORD 33
**Lead:** ice is an ingredient and melting is dilution you did not choose — chill everything before it
meets ice so the ice HOLDS the cold rather than creating it · season the liquid in a jug because a built
glass cannot be stirred · make it taste too strong because ice will dilute it by design · spoon the
avocado ragged, it is a texture not a fruit · crushing multiplies surface area (Es Teler).
**Moat:** the Boston ice trade — worthless frozen pond water made valuable purely by being moved, and the
whole business resting on the fact that melting happens only at the surface, so a big block is insulated
by its own bulk (register COMMERCIAL / INDUSTRIAL HISTORY + geometry). Second beat: avocado is ~15% fat
with almost no sugar or acid, nearly the composition of cream, and the savoury-only convention is the
historical outlier (Es Teler default).
**Ownership added:** ice behaviour and dilution = Es Teler · avocado = Es Teler · the Boston ice trade =
Es Teler. ⚠️ Cendol keeps the droplet, the ice-set and the never-mixed rule; the two cards now cite each
other as opposites.

## ⛔ SERUNDENG STRUCK — THE SECOND DEAD QUEUE ENTRY IN SIX RECORDS

Record 42 was going to be Serundeng. It cannot be written, and unlike Kue Putu it is not owned by
one card but by **three**:
- **Urap-Urap's version 3 is literally `"Serundeng Kelapa — Dry-Fried Golden Coconut"` (R31)**, and
  `"Serundeng Kelapa"` is already an alias on that record. The name is taken.
- **Rendang owns the dry toast in full** — *white → pale cream → biscuit → deep nutty brown, it moves
  fast at the end, the last thirty seconds are where it burns, pull it at dark toast not coffee, and
  tip it onto a COLD plate because a hot pan carries on cooking it.* There is nothing left to teach.
- **Sate Lilit** owns dry coconut as structure; **Bawang Goreng** owns lift-it-pale carryover.

## ✅ RECORD 42 — DENDENG BALADO (`indonesia-dendeng-balado`) · main · BANKED

**Gates:** `node --check` ✅ · `merge` 41 + 1 = 42 ✅ (all assertions, zero warns) ·
`pricecheck` exact **91** · absent 0 · **wrong-product 0** ✅ · `costcheck` **126/126 ✅** ·
`wowcheck` green ✅ · `tinza-doctor` RED **10** (floor). Batch merged, `rm`'d, re-checked ✅.
**costPP engine-derived (§30.1):** R29 · R49 · R56.
⚠️ **A stray non-Latin character was caught in the draft and removed before merging** — a `食` had
slipped into the default version's trivia. Found by scanning the batch for non-emoji CJK before the
merge, not by eye. Worth keeping that scan in the routine.

**THE LEAD (new):** ⚖️ **YOU ARE NOT TENDERISING THE BEEF, YOU ARE CHANGING ITS GEOMETRY SO IT CAN
DRY.** Drying is limited entirely by **how far water has to travel to reach a surface** — water in the
middle of a 10mm slice never all gets out, because the outside case-hardens and traps the rest;
water in the middle of a 2mm sheet is practically already at the surface.
⚖️ **AND IT IS THE EXACT INVERSE OF SATE KLATHAK, WHICH IS WHY THE PAIR READS WELL TOGETHER.** That
card cuts 3–4cm chunks *because a steel skewer delivers heat into the middle*. Nothing conducts
anything into the middle here — **so the only way to deal with the centre is to abolish it.** Two
cards, opposite instructions, one underlying question: how far does heat, or water, have to travel.
- **Slice ACROSS the grain** — short fibres shatter, long fibres bend and pull. Part-freeze 20 min
  first for even slices, and evenness matters more here than almost anywhere.
- ⛔ **Beat with the FLAT face, never the spiked tenderiser** — spikes sever fibres and hole the
  sheet, and a holed sheet tears apart in the oil.
- ⛔ **Do not fry a wet marinated sheet**, which is what most home attempts do: it boils rather than
  fries, holds at 100°C, goes grey and leathery and never recovers. ⚖️ Ayam Tangkap is the same
  physics from the other side — *a leaf can be dried IN the pan because it has no interior; meat has
  one, so the drying happens BEFORE the pan.* That is why there is a three-hour step in the middle.
- ⚠️ **Reduce the balado until the oil visibly separates** — that separation is the doneness signal,
  not a garnish: it means the water has gone, and a watery sambal sogs a crisp sheet in a minute.
- **Fry 40–60 seconds, lift the moment the fierce bubbling stops.**
- ✅ **THE TEST IS THAT IT SHATTERS.** If it bends, the DRYING was short, not the frying — ⛔ and
  frying it longer only burns it. Put the rest back to dry another hour.
- **Coat at the last second**: five minutes before the sambal starts softening it. *Balado means it
  wears the chilli. Serve it wearing the chilli, and serve it fast.*

**THE MOAT (register: CONVERGENT TECHNOLOGY / FOOD SCIENCE):** almost every culture with more meat
than it could eat in a week arrived independently at drying — biltong, jerky, charqui, pastirma,
bakkwa, dendeng — and none borrowed it from the others. ✅ **What differs is the route, and dendeng
is one of the very few that adds a second act: it is dried like the others and then FRIED**, which is
what turns a chewy preserved food into something that shatters. ⚖️ And the science is not what people
assume: **salt and sugar do not poison bacteria, they take the WATER away from them.** Microbes cannot
use water that is chemically bound, so what matters is *water activity* — free water on a scale where
pure water is 1.0 — and below roughly **0.85** essentially nothing that spoils food can grow, however
much total water is present. That one number explains why honey never goes off, why jam is safe until
opened, why a fruitcake keeps a year, and **why a heavily sweetened dendeng is exactly as safe as a
heavily salted one.** It also names the failure mode: a sheet left too thick is dry outside and above
0.85 in the middle. *Every millimetre you take off the sheet is water activity you can actually
reach.*
⚠️ **Checked against Rendang's moat before writing:** Rendang owns **merantau and preservation-as-
migration** — food that travels with people. This is the physics of why drying works and how the
world's dried meats differ. Different mechanism, different story, and merantau is not mentioned.
⚖️ **Deliberately written GLOBAL per the §GLOBAL-WORDING rule** — biltong is named as one of six
equals rather than privileged, so the paragraph needs no rewrite for another locale even though it
will land hardest for a South African reader.

🌱 **NUDGE — A LIME OR LEMON TREE**, the longest-payback one in the lane. Citrus does genuinely well
in Pretoria: the Highveld's cold winter nights are what citrus needs to colour and sweeten, and the
summers are long enough to ripen. ⚠️ **The one thing to get right is frost while it is young** — the
first two or three winters are when a citrus dies, so morning sun, a wall behind it, mulched roots,
or keep it potted and move it. ⚠️ **Buy a grafted nursery tree, not a pip** — a pip takes most of a
decade and often does not come true. ✅ Then close to free fruit for decades, and there is no
ingredient in this cuisine a lime does not improve.

**Versions:** 💰 *Dendeng Rumahan* (R29, leads — trivia: **concentrated, crunchy, strongly seasoned
food eats bigger than its weight**, which is why almost every cuisine with a history of scarcity has
a crisp salty preserved thing in it. ⚠️ And an honest consequence flagged: losing the jaggery costs a
little *preservation* as well as flavour, so the jar keeps four or five days rather than a week, and
the sheet wants slightly longer drying because less sugar means more free water) · 🥩 *Dendeng Balado
— Beaten Thin, Dried, Fried, Coated* (default, R49 — trivia is the sibling that takes the opposite
decision: **dendeng batokok** is beaten too (*batokok* means beaten) but grilled rather than dried and
fried, so it stays thick, chewy and juicy under a green sambal. ✅ *Same cut, same mallet, same region,
opposite result — which proves the beating was never about tenderness, because if it were the two
would taste much the same.* One shatters, one yields, **you cannot have both, and pretending
otherwise is how people end up with beef that is neither**) · 🫙 *Dendeng Kering — Double-Fried for the
Jar* (R56 — **frying twice to make it keep is not a contradiction**: the first fry takes the free
surface water, the 20-minute REST lets the moisture still in the centre migrate outward and even
itself out, and the second cooler fry removes that too. ✅ *One long fry cannot do it, because the
outside burns before the middle has time to move its water anywhere* — the twice-cooked-chip
mechanism applied to meat. ⛔ These are not coated; the sambal goes alongside so the jar stays crisp).

**⚠️ STORAGE HAS A SPLIT PERSONALITY AND SAYS SO.** ⛔ Coated: five good minutes, soft within the
hour, no way back. ✅ **Uncoated in a properly airtight jar: a week at room temperature** — and
⚠️ *completely cool* is not a suggestion, a jar closed on warm beef sweats and the batch is limp by
morning. ⛔ **Do not refrigerate the fried sheets** — a fridge is damp and they soften faster in there
than on the counter. Sambal keeps 2 weeks under its own oil, freezes 3 months. *Run the dish as two
halves and marry them a plateful at a time.*

## ⚖️ WHY 42 AND NOT 50 — THE MEASUREMENT BEHIND THE CALL

**The canon is covered.** 42 records plus **126 versions** already hold Gudeg, Karedok, Nasi Kuning,
Dawet Ayu, Serundeng Kelapa, Bebek Betutu, Sate Tempe, Rendang Tempe, Klepon Ubi, Pepes Tahu, Rawon
Rusuk, Soto Betawi Jeroan and the rest — **many famous dishes are in the file as forks rather than
records**, which is exactly what A3's three-version rule was designed to do.

⚖️ **That is WHY the queue kept dying, and the pattern only became visible in hindsight:** Kue Putu
was Klepon · Serundeng was Urap-Urap's v3 · Gudeg was already record 12 · Bebek Betutu was Ayam
Betutu's v3 · Martabak Manis and Lapis Legit hit the egg ceiling · Nasi Liwet had no fourth rice lead
· Rujak Cingur (petis), Bubur Ketan Hitam (black glutinous rice) and Sate Kelinci (rabbit → chicken)
are all blocked on prices. **Six of the last ten candidates were already in the file.**

## ⚖️ LEMONGRASS — SOURCED BY TINA 30 Jul 2026. **THE KEY WAS ALREADY RIGHT.**
Her figures: **Woolworths fresh 30g pack R14.99** · fresh stalks R15–R20 per bunch · dried cut 75g R79.
- R14.99 ÷ 30g = **R499.67/kg**, and the live key is **`"lemongrass": 500`**. It was correct, and it is
  now **sourced rather than assumed** — attribution written into `prices.js:986` with a dated
  `PRICE_LEDGER.json` entry. **93 keys / 93 entries, check green.**
- ⚖️ Worth recording that this is the pleasant case: a number carried on trust turned out to be right when
  finally measured against a real shelf. The 176-prep-in-name figure did the same thing earlier today.
  Verification is not only for finding errors.
- 🟡 **`dried lemongrass` aliases to the fresh key but is a different product** — Tina priced dried at
  R79/75g = **R1053/kg**, roughly double. **Zero live ingredient lines use it**, so it is latent, not
  shipping. Flagged in-file, deliberately not "fixed" — adding a `dried lemongrass` key with no live
  consumer is churn.
- ⛔ **Landmine re-stated in the file itself:** `4 lemongrass stalks` → **R0.00, coverage 0.00**. A weight
  key cannot price a count line. **ALWAYS WRITE GRAMS.**

## 🔴 THE LEMON FINDING — AND TWO CORRECTIONS TO MY OWN FRAMING OF IT
Probing lemon substitutes surfaced something live and cross-lane. **I described it wrongly twice before
measuring it properly, and both corrections are recorded here rather than quietly dropped.**

**Claim 1, WRONG:** *"`lemons` and `lemon zest` price a whole lemon at 96 cents — an 8× underprice."*
**Claim 2, WRONG:** *"this is a count-vs-weight bug."*

**What is actually true, measured across every recipe file:**

| lemon lines (excluding `lemon juice` and `preserved lemon`) | count |
|---|---|
| ✅ price correctly (≥ R1) | **29** |
| 🟠 priced but effectively free (R0 < x < R1) | **0** |
| 🔵 **blank — R0.00** | **35** |

- **Gram lines are fine.** `30g lemon` prices correctly because `AVG_WEIGHT_G` bridges a weight line onto
  the count key — the same mechanism that was verified this morning on `30g avocado`. The "effectively
  free" band is **empty**, which is the measurement that disproves my own claim.
- **Count lines are fine.** `1 lemon` → R8.00.
- **The real failure is 35 QUANTITY-LESS lines** — `lemon`, `lemon wedges`, `Lemon zest`,
  `lemon (serving)` — carrying no number at all, so nothing can price them. Plus a handful of
  prep-in-the-name cases like `1 lemon, cut in wedges` (`wk_china.js`), which is the carried 176-line
  problem biting in a measurable way.
- ⚖️ **This is the GAP rung, not the WRONG rung.** A blank announces itself; it does not render a
  plausible number. So **§29.5 does not force an immediate fix** and it batches like any other gap.
- ⛔ **NOT ACTED ON.** The 35 lines are concentrated in `wk_africa.js` and `wk_europe.js`, entirely
  outside the Indonesia lane. **Stability Rule 1: never edit a working section unless that IS the session
  purpose.** Logged with a hard number so a future pass can start from a measurement rather than a hunt.

⚖️ **THE LESSON, and it is the same one as the wowcheck hole earlier today:** the first two descriptions
were reasoned from how the keys *looked* rather than from running the cost engine over the actual lines.
Both were plausible and both were wrong, and the correct answer was three commands away. **Measure the
thing, then describe it.** Reasoning about a price table is not the same as pricing a line.

### 🆕 THREE THINGS FOUND THIS BANK

1. 🩸 **`merge.js` FALSE\_FRIENDS IS MISSING `coconut cream`.** `thailand-khao-niao-mamuang`
   is correctly tagged vegan, but the vegan-mistag rung strips `coconut milk` from the
   ingredients before matching and **does not strip `coconut cream`** — so the bare word
   `cream` hits the DAIRY\_EGG list and it warns on a correct record, every merge.
   ⚖️ **Same family as the FLESH list missing octopus + dashi.** A rung that fires on a
   correct record is the rung she learns to scroll past. **One word closes it:**
   `FALSE_FRIENDS` in `merge.js` → add `'coconut cream'` **before** `'coconut milk'`.
   ⛔ **Not done in this bank** — it is an edit to the gate itself, and `merge-selftest.js`
   must be re-run and given a born-RED proof. **Tina's call.**
2. ⚠️ **`preserved radish` (chai poh) prices as FRESH `radish` R108** — a wrong-product risk
   the cold start had not probed. Kept **out** of Pad Thai's ingredient line and named
   in-method instead, so nothing renders a wrong number.

   **says so on the card** rather than substituting silently.
